\title{Hall's marriage theorem}
\author{}
\date{}
\begin{document}
\maketitle
\subsection*{Prerequisites}
\begin{description}
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:436c6173736963616c2e63686f6f73655f73706563}\paperlink{premise:436c6173736963616c2e63686f6f73655f73706563}{P1.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:436c6173736963616c2e63686f6f73655f73706563}\textbf{Choose spec.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Let \(h\) be a proof of \(\exists x\, :\, \alpha,\; p\left(x\right)\). Then \(p\left(\text{the fixed choice from }\{x\in \alpha \mid p(x)\}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:436c6173736963616c2e6e6f745f6e6f74}\paperlink{premise:436c6173736963616c2e6e6f745f6e6f74}{P2.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:436c6173736963616c2e6e6f745f6e6f74}\textbf{Not not.} Let \(a\) be a proposition. Then \(\neg \left(\neg a\right) \iff a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:45712e6c65}\paperlink{premise:45712e6c65}{P3.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:45712e6c65}\textbf{Equality implies a weak inequality.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a preorder. Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Assume \(a = b\). Then \(a \le b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e6974652e6f665f66696e74797065}\paperlink{premise:46696e6974652e6f665f66696e74797065}{P4.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e6974652e6f665f66696e74797065}\textbf{Of fintype.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a finite enumeration of \(\alpha\). Then \(\operatorname{Finite}\left(\alpha\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6269556e696f6e5f7375627365745f6269556e696f6e5f6f665f7375627365745f6c656674}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6269556e696f6e5f7375627365745f6269556e696f6e5f6f665f7375627365745f6c656674}{P5.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6269556e696f6e5f7375627365745f6269556e696f6e5f6f665f7375627365745f6c656674}\textbf{Bi union subset bi union of subset left.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Let \(s_{1}\) and \(s_{2}\) be finite subsets of \(\alpha\). Assume equality on \(\beta\) is decidable. Let \(t\) be a family of finite subsets of \(\beta\) indexed by \(\alpha\). Assume \(s_{1} \subseteq s_{2}\). Then \(\bigcup_{i\in s_{1}} t(i) \subseteq \bigcup_{i\in s_{2}} t(i)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f636f6d706c5f6c745f6966665f6e6f6e656d707479}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f636f6d706c5f6c745f6966665f6e6f6e656d707479}{P6.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f636f6d706c5f6c745f6966665f6e6f6e656d707479}\textbf{Cardinality compl less than if and only if nonempty.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a finite enumeration of \(\alpha\). Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Then \(\left|s^{\mathrm c}\right| < \left|\alpha\right| \iff s\ne\varnothing\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f65726173655f6f665f6d656d}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f65726173655f6f665f6d656d}{P7.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f65726173655f6f665f6d656d}\textbf{Cardinality erase of membership.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Assume \(a \in s\). Then \(\left|s \setminus \{a\}\right| = \left|s\right| - 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f696d6167655f6f665f696e6a656374697665}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f696d6167655f6f665f696e6a656374697665}{P8.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f696d6167655f6f665f696e6a656374697665}\textbf{Cardinality image of injective.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \beta\). Assume equality on \(\beta\) is decidable. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Assume \(f\text{ is injective}\). Then \(\left|f\left[s\right]\right| = \left|s\right|\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f6c655f63617264}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f6c655f63617264}{P9.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f6c655f63617264}\textbf{Cardinality at most cardinality.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(s\) and \(t\) be finite subsets of \(\alpha\). Assume \(s \subseteq t\). Then \(\left|s\right| \le \left|t\right|\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f6c745f6966665f6e655f756e6976}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f6c745f6966665f6e655f756e6976}{P10.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f6c745f6966665f6e655f756e6976}\textbf{Cardinality less than if and only if differs from whole space.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a finite enumeration of \(\alpha\). Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Then \(\left|s\right| < \left|\alpha\right| \iff s \ne \alpha\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f706f73}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f706f73}{P11.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f706f73}\textbf{Cardinality positivity.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Then \(0 < \left|s\right| \iff s\ne\varnothing\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f7364696666}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f7364696666}{P12.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f7364696666}\textbf{Cardinality sdiff.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(s\) and \(t\) be finite subsets of \(\alpha\). Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Assume \(s \subseteq t\). Then \(\left|t \setminus s\right| = \left|t\right| - \left|s\right|\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f756e696f6e5f6f665f6469736a6f696e74}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f756e696f6e5f6f665f6469736a6f696e74}{P13.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e636172645f756e696f6e5f6f665f6469736a6f696e74}\textbf{Cardinality union of disjoint.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(s\) and \(t\) be finite subsets of \(\alpha\). Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Assume \(\operatorname{Disjoint}\left(s, t\right)\). Then \(\left|s \cup t\right| = \left|s\right| + \left|t\right|\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e636f655f636f6d706c}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e636f655f636f6d706c}{P14.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e636f655f636f6d706c}\textbf{Coercion compl.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a finite enumeration of \(\alpha\). Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Then \(\operatorname{underlying\,set}(s^{\mathrm c}) = s^{\mathrm c}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6469736a6f696e745f6c656674}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6469736a6f696e745f6c656674}{P15.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6469736a6f696e745f6c656674}\textbf{Disjoint left.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(s\) and \(t\) be finite subsets of \(\alpha\). Then \(\operatorname{Disjoint}(s,\allowbreak{} t) \iff (\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; a \in s \implies a \notin t)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f6269556e696f6e}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f6269556e696f6e}{P16.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f6269556e696f6e}\textbf{Erase bi union.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Assume equality on \(\beta\) is decidable. Let \(f\) be a family of finite subsets of \(\beta\) indexed by \(\alpha\). Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Let \(b\) be an element of \(\beta\). Then \((\bigcup_{i\in s} f(i)) \setminus \{b\} = \bigcup_{x\in s} (f(x) \setminus \{b\})\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f65715f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f65715f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}{P17.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e65726173655f65715f6f665f6e6f745f6d656d}\textbf{Erase equals of not membership.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Assume \(a \notin s\). Then \(s \setminus \{a\} = s\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e657874}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e657874}{P18.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e657874}\textbf{Ext.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(s_{1}\) and \(s_{2}\) be finite subsets of \(\alpha\). Assume \(\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; a \in s_{1} \iff a \in s_{2}\). Then \(s_{1} = s_{2}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e696d6167655f6e6f6e656d707479}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e696d6167655f6e6f6e656d707479}{P19.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e696d6167655f6e6f6e656d707479}\textbf{Image nonempty.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Assume equality on \(\beta\) is decidable. Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \beta\). Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Then \(f\left[s\right]\ne\varnothing \iff s\ne\varnothing\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6269556e696f6e}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6269556e696f6e}{P20.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6269556e696f6e}\textbf{Membership bi union.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Let \(t\) be a family of finite subsets of \(\beta\) indexed by \(\alpha\). Assume equality on \(\beta\) is decidable. Let \(b\) be an element of \(\beta\). Then \(b \in \bigcup_{i\in s} t(i) \iff \left(\exists a\, :\, \alpha,\; a \in s \land b \in t\left(a\right)\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6572617365}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6572617365}{P21.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6572617365}\textbf{Membership erase.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Then \(a \in s \setminus \{b\} \iff a \ne b \land a \in s\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f696d616765}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f696d616765}{P22.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f696d616765}\textbf{Membership image.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Assume equality on \(\beta\) is decidable. Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \beta\). Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Let \(b\) be an element of \(\beta\). Then \(b \in f\left[s\right] \iff \left(\exists a\, :\, \alpha,\; a \in s \land f\left(a\right) = b\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f7364696666}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f7364696666}{P23.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f7364696666}\textbf{Membership sdiff.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Let \(s\) and \(t\) be finite subsets of \(\alpha\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(a \in s \setminus t \iff a \in s \land a \notin t\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f756e696f6e}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f756e696f6e}{P24.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f756e696f6e}\textbf{Membership union.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Let \(s\) and \(t\) be finite subsets of \(\alpha\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(a \in s \cup t \iff a \in s \lor a \in t\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f756e6976}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f756e6976}{P25.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f756e6976}\textbf{Membership in the full set.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a finite enumeration of \(\alpha\). Let \(x\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(x \in \alpha\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e6e6f6e656d7074795f6966665f6e655f656d707479}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6e6f6e656d7074795f6966665f6e655f656d707479}{P26.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e6e6f6e656d7074795f6966665f6e655f656d707479}\textbf{Nonempty if and only if differs from empty.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Then \(s\ne\varnothing \iff s \ne \varnothing\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e73646966665f737562736574}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e73646966665f737562736574}{P27.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e73646966665f737562736574}\textbf{Sdiff subset.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Let \(s\) and \(t\) be finite subsets of \(\alpha\). Then \(s \setminus t \subseteq s\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e73696e676c65746f6e5f6269556e696f6e}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e73696e676c65746f6e5f6269556e696f6e}{P28.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e73696e676c65746f6e5f6269556e696f6e}\textbf{Singleton bi union.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Let \(t\) be a family of finite subsets of \(\beta\) indexed by \(\alpha\). Assume equality on \(\beta\) is decidable. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(\bigcup_{i\in \{a\}} t(i) = t\left(a\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e7365742e7375627365745f756e696f6e5f6c656674}\paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e7375627365745f756e696f6e5f6c656674}{P29.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e7365742e7375627365745f756e696f6e5f6c656674}\textbf{Subset union left.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Let \(s_{1}\) and \(s_{2}\) be finite subsets of \(\alpha\). Then \(s_{1} \subseteq s_{1} \cup s_{2}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f636f65}\paperlink{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f636f65}{P30.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f636f65}\textbf{Cardinality coercion.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(s\) be a finite subset of \(\alpha\). Fix a finite enumeration of \(s\). Then \(\left|s\right| = \left|s\right|\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f636f6e677227}\paperlink{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f636f6e677227}{P31.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f636f6e677227}\textbf{Cardinality congr'.} Let \(\alpha\) and \(\beta\) be types. Fix a finite enumeration of \(\alpha\). Fix a finite enumeration of \(\beta\). Assume \(\alpha = \beta\). Then \(\left|\alpha\right| = \left|\beta\right|\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f65715f7a65726f5f696666}\paperlink{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f65715f7a65726f5f696666}{P32.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f65715f7a65726f5f696666}\textbf{Cardinality equals zero if and only if.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a finite enumeration of \(\alpha\). Then \(\left|\alpha\right| = 0 \iff \alpha\text{ is empty}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f706f735f696666}\paperlink{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f706f735f696666}{P33.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46696e747970652e636172645f706f735f696666}\textbf{Cardinality positivity if and only if.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a finite enumeration of \(\alpha\). Then \(0 < \left|\alpha\right| \iff \alpha\text{ is nonempty}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46756e6374696f6e2e496e6a6563746976652e64697465}\paperlink{premise:46756e6374696f6e2e496e6a6563746976652e64697465}{P34.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46756e6374696f6e2e496e6a6563746976652e64697465}\textbf{Dite.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(\beta\) be a sort. Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Assume the predicate \(p\) is decidable. Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\{a\in \alpha \mid p\left(a\right)\} \to \beta\). Let \(f^{'}\) be a function of type \(\{a\in \alpha \mid \neg p\left(a\right)\} \to \beta\). Assume \(f\text{ is injective}\). Assume \(f^{'}\text{ is injective}\). Assume \(\forall x\, :\, \alpha,\; \forall x^{'}\, :\, \alpha,\; \forall \mathit{hx}\, :\, p(x),\; \forall h_{1}\, :\, \neg p(x^{'}),\; f((x\, :\, \{a\in \alpha \mid p(a)\})) \ne f^{'}((x^{'}\, :\, \{a\in \alpha \mid \neg p(a)\}))\). Then \((x \mapsto \begin{cases}f((x\, :\, \{a\in \alpha \mid p(a)\})) &\text{given } h_{1}\text{ proving } p(x)\\ f^{'}((x\, :\, \{a\in \alpha \mid \neg p(a)\})) &\text{given } h_{1}\text{ proving } \neg p(x)\end{cases})\text{ is injective}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:46756e6374696f6e2e496e6a6563746976652e65715f696666}\paperlink{premise:46756e6374696f6e2e496e6a6563746976652e65715f696666}{P35.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:46756e6374696f6e2e496e6a6563746976652e65715f696666}\textbf{Equals if and only if.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(\beta\) be a sort. Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \beta\). Assume \(f\text{ is injective}\). Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Then \(f\left(a\right) = f\left(b\right) \iff a = b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4966662e6f665f6571}\paperlink{premise:4966662e6f665f6571}{P36.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4966662e6f665f6571}\textbf{Of equals.} Let \(a\) and \(b\) be propositions. Assume \(a = b\). Then \(a \iff b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4973456d7074792e6578697374735f696666}\paperlink{premise:4973456d7074792e6578697374735f696666}{P37.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4973456d7074792e6578697374735f696666}\textbf{Exists if and only if.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Assume \(\alpha\text{ is empty}\). Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Then \(\left(\exists a\, :\, \alpha,\; p\left(a\right)\right) \iff \mathrm{False}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4973456d7074792e666f72616c6c5f696666}\paperlink{premise:4973456d7074792e666f72616c6c5f696666}{P38.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4973456d7074792e666f72616c6c5f696666}\textbf{Forall if and only if.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Assume \(\alpha\text{ is empty}\). Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Then \(\left(\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; p\left(a\right)\right) \iff \mathrm{True}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4c452e6c652e7472616e73}\paperlink{premise:4c452e6c652e7472616e73}{P39.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4c452e6c652e7472616e73}\textbf{Trans.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a preorder. Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Assume \(a \le b\). Assume \(b \le c\). Then \(a \le c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e507573684e65672e6e6f745f666f72616c6c5f6571}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e507573684e65672e6e6f745f666f72616c6c5f6571}{P40.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e507573684e65672e6e6f745f666f72616c6c5f6571}\textbf{Not forall equals.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(s\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Then \((\neg (\forall x\, :\, \alpha,\; s(x))) = (\exists x\, :\, \alpha,\; \neg s(x))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e507573684e65672e6e6f745f696d706c6965735f6571}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e507573684e65672e6e6f745f696d706c6965735f6571}{P41.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e507573684e65672e6e6f745f696d706c6965735f6571}\textbf{Not implies equals.} Let \(p\) and \(q\) be propositions. Then \(\left(\neg \left(p \implies q\right)\right) = \left(p \land \neg q\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e507573684e65672e6e6f745f6c745f6571}\paperlink{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e507573684e65672e6e6f745f6c745f6571}{P42.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4d6174686c69622e5461637469632e507573684e65672e6e6f745f6c745f6571}\textbf{Not less than equals.} Let \(\beta\) be a type. Equip \(\beta\) with a linear order. Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\beta\). Then \(\left(\neg \left(a < b\right)\right) = \left(b \le a\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6164645f6f6e65}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6164645f6f6e65}{P43.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6164645f6f6e65}\textbf{Sum one.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(n + 1 = n + 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6164645f7375625f63616e63656c5f6c656674}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6164645f7375625f63616e63656c5f6c656674}{P44.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6164645f7375625f63616e63656c5f6c656674}\textbf{Sum difference cancel left.} Let \(n\) and \(m\) be natural numbers. Then \(n + m - n = m\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6164645f737563635f7375625f6f6e65}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6164645f737563635f7375625f6f6e65}{P45.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6164645f737563635f7375625f6f6e65}\textbf{Sum successor difference one.} Let \(m\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. Then \(m + \left(n + 1\right) - 1 = m + n\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6c655f616e746973796d6d}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6c655f616e746973796d6d}{P46.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6c655f616e746973796d6d}\textbf{At most antisymm.} Let \(n\) and \(m\) be natural numbers. If \(n \le m\) and \(m \le n\), then \(n = m\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6c655f6f665f6c74}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6c655f6f665f6c74}{P47.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6c655f6f665f6c74}\textbf{At most of less than.} Let \(n\) and \(m\) be natural numbers. If \(n < m\), then \(n \le m\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6c655f6f665f6c745f73756363}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6c655f6f665f6c745f73756363}{P48.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6c655f6f665f6c745f73756363}\textbf{At most of less than successor.} Let \(m\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. If \(m < n + 1\), then \(m \le n\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6c655f7375625f6f6e655f6f665f6c74}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6c655f7375625f6f6e655f6f665f6c74}{P49.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6c655f7375625f6f6e655f6f665f6c74}\textbf{At most difference one of less than.} Let \(a\) and \(b\) be natural numbers. Assume \(a < b\). Then \(a \le b - 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6c745f737563635f696666}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6c745f737563635f696666}{P50.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6c745f737563635f696666}\textbf{Less than successor if and only if.} Let \(m\) and \(n\) be natural numbers. Then \(m < n + 1 \iff m \le n\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e6c745f737563635f6f665f6c65}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e6c745f737563635f6f665f6c65}{P51.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e6c745f737563635f6f665f6c65}\textbf{Less than successor of at most.} Let \(n\) and \(m\) be natural numbers. If \(n \le m\), then \(n < m + 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e7375625f6c655f7375625f7269676874}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e7375625f6c655f7375625f7269676874}{P52.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e7375625f6c655f7375625f7269676874}\textbf{Difference at most difference right.} Let \(n\) and \(m\) be natural numbers. Assume \(n \le m\). Let \(k\) be a natural number. Then \(n - k \le m - k\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e61742e737563635f706f73}\paperlink{premise:4e61742e737563635f706f73}{P53.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e61742e737563635f706f73}\textbf{Successor positivity.} Let \(n\) be a natural number. Then \(0 < n + 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:4e652e73796d6d}\paperlink{premise:4e652e73796d6d}{P54.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:4e652e73796d6d}\textbf{Symm.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(a\) and \(b\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Assume \(a \ne b\). Then \(b \ne a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:5365742e636172645f6e655f6571}\paperlink{premise:5365742e636172645f6e655f6571}{P55.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:5365742e636172645f6e655f6571}\textbf{Cardinality differs from equals.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Fix a finite enumeration of \(\alpha\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Fix a finite enumeration of \(\{x\in \alpha \mid x \ne a\}\). Then \(\left|\{x\in \alpha \mid x \ne a\}\right| = \left|\alpha\right| - 1\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:536574436f652e657869737473}\paperlink{premise:536574436f652e657869737473}{P56.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:536574436f652e657869737473}\textbf{Exists.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Let \(s\) be a subset of \(\alpha\). Let \(p\) be a function of type \(s \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Then \((\exists x\, :\, s,\; p(x)) \iff (\exists x\, :\, \alpha,\; \exists h\text{ proving }(x \in s),\; p((x\, :\, s)))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:537562747970652e636f655f696e6a656374697665}\paperlink{premise:537562747970652e636f655f696e6a656374697665}{P57.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:537562747970652e636f655f696e6a656374697665}\textbf{Coercion injective.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Then \(\left(a \mapsto a\right)\text{ is injective}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:537562747970652e657869737473}\paperlink{premise:537562747970652e657869737473}{P58.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:537562747970652e657869737473}\textbf{Exists.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Let \(q\) be a function of type \(\{a\in \alpha \mid p\left(a\right)\} \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Then \((\exists x\, :\, \{a\in \alpha \mid p(a)\},\; q(x)) \iff (\exists a\, :\, \alpha,\; \exists b\text{ proving }p(a),\; q((a\, :\, \{a_{1}\in \alpha \mid p(a_{1})\})))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:537562747970652e6d6b2e696e6a4571}\paperlink{premise:537562747970652e6d6b2e696e6a4571}{P59.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:537562747970652e6d6b2e696e6a4571}\textbf{Injectivity equals.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Let \(\mathit{val}\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(\mathit{property}\) be a proof of \(p\left(\mathit{val}\right)\). Let \(\mathit{val}_{1}\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Let \(\mathit{property}_{1}\) be a proof of \(p\left(\mathit{val}_{1}\right)\). Then \(((\mathit{val}\, :\, \{x\in \alpha \mid p(x)\}) = (\mathit{val}_{1}\, :\, \{x\in \alpha \mid p(x)\})) = (\mathit{val} = \mathit{val}_{1})\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6164645f7a65726f}\paperlink{premise:6164645f7a65726f}{P60.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6164645f7a65726f}\textbf{Sum zero.} Let \(M\) be a type. Fix a structure \(\mathit{inst}\) of type \(\operatorname{Add\,Zero\,Class}\left(M\right)\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(M\). Then \(a + 0 = a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:616e645f696d70}\paperlink{premise:616e645f696d70}{P61.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:616e645f696d70}\textbf{And imp.} Let \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\) be propositions. Then \(a \land b \implies c \iff a \implies b \implies c\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:636f6e67725f617267}\paperlink{premise:636f6e67725f617267}{P62.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:636f6e67725f617267}\textbf{Congruence arg.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(\beta\) be a sort. Let \(a_{1}\) and \(a_{2}\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \beta\). Assume \(a_{1} = a_{2}\). Then \(f\left(a_{1}\right) = f\left(a_{2}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6469665f6e6567}\paperlink{premise:6469665f6e6567}{P63.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6469665f6e6567}\textbf{Dif neg.} Let \(c\) be a proposition. Let \(h\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Decidable}\left(c\right)\). Let \(\mathit{hnc}\) be a proof of \(\neg c\). Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(t\) be a function of type \(c \to \alpha\). Let \(e\) be a function of type \(\neg c \to \alpha\). Then \(\begin{cases}t(h_{1}) &\text{given } h_{1}\text{ proving } c\\ e(h_{1}) &\text{given } h_{1}\text{ proving } \neg c\end{cases} = e(\mathit{hnc})\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6469665f706f73}\paperlink{premise:6469665f706f73}{P64.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6469665f706f73}\textbf{Dif positivity.} Let \(c\) be a proposition. Let \(h\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Decidable}\left(c\right)\). Let \(\mathit{hc}\) be a proof of \(c\). Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(t\) be a function of type \(c \to \alpha\). Let \(e\) be a function of type \(\neg c \to \alpha\). Then \(\begin{cases}t(h_{1}) &\text{given } h_{1}\text{ proving } c\\ e(h_{1}) &\text{given } h_{1}\text{ proving } \neg c\end{cases} = t(\mathit{hc})\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:646974655f636f6e645f65715f66616c7365}\paperlink{premise:646974655f636f6e645f65715f66616c7365}{P65.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:646974655f636f6e645f65715f66616c7365}\textbf{Dite cond equals false.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(c\) be a proposition. Let \(x\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Decidable}\left(c\right)\). Let \(t\) be a function of type \(c \to \alpha\). Let \(e\) be a function of type \(\neg c \to \alpha\). Let \(h\) be a proof of \(c = \mathrm{False}\). Then \(\begin{cases}t(h_{1}) &\text{given } h_{1}\text{ proving } c\\ e(h_{1}) &\text{given } h_{1}\text{ proving } \neg c\end{cases} = e(\operatorname{of\,eq\,false}(h))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:646974655f636f6e645f65715f74727565}\paperlink{premise:646974655f636f6e645f65715f74727565}{P66.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:646974655f636f6e645f65715f74727565}\textbf{Dite cond equals true.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(c\) be a proposition. Let \(x\) be an element of \(\operatorname{Decidable}\left(c\right)\). Let \(t\) be a function of type \(c \to \alpha\). Let \(e\) be a function of type \(\neg c \to \alpha\). Let \(h\) be a proof of \(c = \mathrm{True}\). Then \(\begin{cases}t(h_{1}) &\text{given } h_{1}\text{ proving } c\\ e(h_{1}) &\text{given } h_{1}\text{ proving } \neg c\end{cases} = t(\operatorname{transport}^{\leftarrow}_{h}(\mathrm{trivial}))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:65715f66616c7365}\paperlink{premise:65715f66616c7365}{P67.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:65715f66616c7365}\textbf{Equals false.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Assume \(\neg p\). Then \(p = \mathrm{False}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:65715f6f665f686571}\paperlink{premise:65715f6f665f686571}{P68.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:65715f6f665f686571}\textbf{Equals of heq.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(a\) and \(a^{'}\) be elements of \(\alpha\). Assume \(\left(a\, :\, \alpha\right)\text{ is heterogeneously equal to }\left(a^{'}\, :\, \alpha\right)\). Then \(a = a^{'}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6578697374735f616e645f7269676874}\paperlink{premise:6578697374735f616e645f7269676874}{P69.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6578697374735f616e645f7269676874}\textbf{Exists and right.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Let \(b\) be a proposition. Then \((\exists x\, :\, \alpha,\; p(x) \land b) \iff (\exists x\, :\, \alpha,\; p(x)) \land b\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6578697374735f65715f7269676874}\paperlink{premise:6578697374735f65715f7269676874}{P70.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6578697374735f65715f7269676874}\textbf{Exists equals right.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Let \(a^{'}\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(\left(\exists a\, :\, \alpha,\; p\left(a\right) \land a = a^{'}\right) \iff p\left(a^{'}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6578697374735f696d70}\paperlink{premise:6578697374735f696d70}{P71.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6578697374735f696d70}\textbf{Exists imp.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Let \(b\) be a proposition. Then \((\exists x\, :\, \alpha,\; p(x)) \implies b \iff (\forall x\, :\, \alpha,\; p(x) \implies b)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6578697374735f70726f705f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:6578697374735f70726f705f636f6e6772}{P72.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6578697374735f70726f705f636f6e6772}\textbf{Exists prop congruence.} Let \(p\) and \(p^{'}\) be propositions. Let \(q\) be a function of type \(p \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Let \(q^{'}\) be a function of type \(p \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Assume \(\forall h\, :\, p,\; q\left(h\right) \iff q^{'}\left(h\right)\). Let \(\mathit{hp}\) be a proof of \(p \iff p^{'}\). Then \((\exists x\, :\, p,\; q(x)) \iff (\exists h\text{ proving }p^{'},\; q^{'}(\operatorname{Iff\mathord{:}mpr}(\mathit{hp},\allowbreak{} h)))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:66616c73655f616e64}\paperlink{premise:66616c73655f616e64}{P73.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:66616c73655f616e64}\textbf{False and.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Then \(\left(\mathrm{False} \land p\right) = \mathrm{False}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e6772}{P74.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:666f72616c6c5f636f6e6772}\textbf{Forall congruence.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(p\) and \(q\) be functions of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Assume \(\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; p\left(a\right) = q\left(a\right)\). Then \((\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; p(a)) = (\forall a\, :\, \alpha,\; q(a))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:66756e657874}\paperlink{premise:66756e657874}{P75.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:66756e657874}\textbf{Function extensionality.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(\beta\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Sort}\). Let \(f\) be a function of type \(\Pi_{x\, :\, \alpha}\, \beta\left(x\right)\). Let \(g\) be a function of type \(\Pi_{x\, :\, \alpha}\, \beta\left(x\right)\). Assume \(\forall x\, :\, \alpha,\; f\left(x\right) = g\left(x\right)\). Then \(f = g\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6966665f73656c66}\paperlink{premise:6966665f73656c66}{P76.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6966665f73656c66}\textbf{If and only if self.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Then \(\left(p \iff p\right) = \mathrm{True}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:696d705f73656c66}\paperlink{premise:696d705f73656c66}{P77.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:696d705f73656c66}\textbf{Imp self.} Let \(a\) be a proposition. Then \(a \implies a \iff \mathrm{True}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:696d706c6965735f636f6e6772}\paperlink{premise:696d706c6965735f636f6e6772}{P78.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:696d706c6965735f636f6e6772}\textbf{Implies congruence.} Let \(p_{1}\) and \(p_{2}\) be sorts. Let \(q_{1}\) and \(q_{2}\) be sorts. Assume \(p_{1} = p_{2}\). Assume \(q_{1} = q_{2}\). Then \(\left(p_{1} \to q_{1}\right) = \left(p_{2} \to q_{2}\right)\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:696e73744973456d70747946616c7365}\paperlink{premise:696e73744973456d70747946616c7365}{P79.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:696e73744973456d70747946616c7365}\textbf{Inst is empty false.} \(\mathrm{False}\text{ is empty}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6c655f7265666c}\paperlink{premise:6c655f7265666c}{P80.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6c655f7265666c}\textbf{At most refl.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a preorder. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(a \le a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6c655f72666c}\paperlink{premise:6c655f72666c}{P81.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6c655f72666c}\textbf{At most rfl.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Equip \(\alpha\) with a preorder. Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Then \(a \le a\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6e6f6e656d7074795f66696e74797065}\paperlink{premise:6e6f6e656d7074795f66696e74797065}{P82.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6e6f6e656d7074795f66696e74797065}\textbf{Nonempty fintype.} Let \(\alpha\) be a type. Assume \(\alpha\) is finite. Then \(\operatorname{Fintype}\left(\alpha\right)\text{ is nonempty}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6e6f745f657869737473}\paperlink{premise:6e6f745f657869737473}{P83.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6e6f745f657869737473}\textbf{Not exists.} Let \(\alpha\) be a sort. Let \(p\) be a function of type \(\alpha \to \mathsf{Prop}\). Then \(\neg (\exists x\, :\, \alpha,\; p(x)) \iff (\forall x\, :\, \alpha,\; \neg p(x))\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6e6f745f747275655f65715f66616c7365}\paperlink{premise:6e6f745f747275655f65715f66616c7365}{P84.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6e6f745f747275655f65715f66616c7365}\textbf{Not true equals false.} \(\left(\neg \mathrm{True}\right) = \mathrm{False}\).
\item[\paperlineanchor{premise:6f665f65715f66616c7365}\paperlink{premise:6f665f65715f66616c7365}{P85.}] \refstepcounter{paperpremise}\label{premise:6f665f65715f66616c7365}\textbf{Of equals false.} Let \(p\) be a proposition. Assume \(p = \mathrm{False}\). Then \(\neg p\).
\end{description}

\section{Finite types}
\begin{lemma}[{Hall condition after deletion}]\label{theorem:1}
Let \(\iota\) and \(\alpha\) be types. Assume equality on \(\alpha\) is decidable. Let \(t\) be a family of finite subsets of \(\alpha\) indexed by \(\iota\). Fix a finite enumeration of \(\iota\). Let \(x\) be an element of \(\iota\). Let \(a\) be an element of \(\alpha\). Assume \(\forall s\, :\, \mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{fin}}(\iota),\; s\ne\varnothing \implies s \ne \iota \implies |s| < |\bigcup_{i\in s} t(i)|\). Let \(s^{'}\) be a finite subset of \(\{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}\). Then
\begin{center}
\(\displaystyle |s^{'}| \le |\bigcup_{x^{'}\in s^{'}} (t(x^{'}) \setminus \{a\})|.\)
\end{center}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Distinguish whether \(s^{'}\ne\varnothing\) holds.
Consider the following case.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(s^{'}\ne\varnothing\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x_{1}\) in \(\alpha\).
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(x_{2}\) in \(\iota\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}\).
By definition, we obtain \(((x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'} \land x_{1} \in t(x_{2})) = ((x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'} \land x_{1} \in t(x_{2}))\).
By \paperlink{premise:4966662e6f665f6571}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4966662e6f665f6571}}, we obtain \((x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'} \land x_{1} \in t(x_{2}) \iff (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'} \land x_{1} \in t(x_{2})\).
\end{proofstep}
By definition, we obtain \((x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) = (x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\})\).
By \paperlink{premise:4966662e6f665f6571}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4966662e6f665f6571}}, we obtain \(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\} \iff x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}\).
By \paperlink{premise:6578697374735f70726f705f636f6e6772}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6578697374735f70726f705f636f6e6772}}, we obtain \((\exists h\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'} \land x_{1} \in t(x_{2})) \iff (\exists h\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'} \land x_{1} \in t(x_{2}))\).
By propositional extensionality, we obtain \((\exists h\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'} \land x_{1} \in t(x_{2})) = (\exists h\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'} \land x_{1} \in t(x_{2}))\).
By \paperlink{premise:6578697374735f616e645f7269676874}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6578697374735f616e645f7269676874}} with \(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}\) as its type parameter \(\alpha\), we obtain \((\exists x_{3}\, :\, x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\},\; (x_{4} \mapsto (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'})(x_{3}) \land x_{1} \in t(x_{2})) \iff (\exists x_{3}\, :\, x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\},\; (x_{4} \mapsto (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'})(x_{3})) \land x_{1} \in t(x_{2})\).
By propositional extensionality, we obtain \((\exists x_{3}\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'} \land x_{1} \in t(x_{2})) = ((\exists x_{3}\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'}) \land x_{1} \in t(x_{2}))\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \((\exists h\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'} \land x_{1} \in t(x_{2})) = ((\exists x_{3}\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'}) \land x_{1} \in t(x_{2}))\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Fix \(a_{1}\) in \(\iota\).
By \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f696d616765}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f696d616765}} with \(\{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}\) as its type parameter \(\alpha\), we obtain \(a_{1} \in (z \mapsto z)[s^{'}] \iff (\exists a_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\},\; a_{2} \in s^{'} \land (z \mapsto z)(a_{2}) = a_{1})\).
By propositional extensionality, we obtain \((a_{1} \in (z \mapsto z)[s^{'}]) = (\exists a_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\},\; a_{2} \in s^{'} \land a_{2} = a_{1})\).
By \paperlink{premise:536574436f652e657869737473}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:536574436f652e657869737473}}, we obtain \((\exists x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\},\; (x_{3} \mapsto x_{3} \in s^{'} \land x_{3} = a_{1})(x_{2})) \iff (\exists x_{2}\, :\, \iota,\; \exists h\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{3} \mapsto x_{3} \in s^{'} \land x_{3} = a_{1})((x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\})))\).
By propositional extensionality, we obtain \((\exists x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\},\; x_{2} \in s^{'} \land x_{2} = a_{1}) = (\exists x_{2}\, :\, \iota,\; \exists h\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'} \land x_{2} = a_{1})\).
For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}

For the required implication, argue as follows.
\begin{proofstep}

By \paperlink{premise:4966662e6f665f6571}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:4966662e6f665f6571}}, we obtain \(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\} \iff x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}\).

By propositional extensionality, we obtain \((\exists h\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'} \land x_{2} = a_{1}) = (\exists h\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'} \land x_{2} = a_{1})\).

By propositional extensionality, we obtain \((\exists x_{3}\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'} \land x_{2} = a_{1}) = ((\exists x_{3}\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'}) \land x_{2} = a_{1})\).

By function extensionality, we obtain \((x_{2} \mapsto \exists h\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'} \land x_{2} = a_{1}) = (x_{2} \mapsto (\exists x_{3}\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'}) \land x_{2} = a_{1})\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \((\exists x_{2}\, :\, \iota,\; \exists h\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'} \land x_{2} = a_{1}) = (\exists x_{2}\, :\, \iota,\; (\exists x_{3}\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'}) \land x_{2} = a_{1})\).
By \paperlink{premise:6578697374735f65715f7269676874}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6578697374735f65715f7269676874}}, we obtain \((\exists a_{2}\, :\, \iota,\; (a_{3} \mapsto \exists x_{2}\text{ proving }(a_{3} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (a_{3}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'})(a_{2}) \land a_{2} = a_{1}) \iff (a_{2} \mapsto \exists x_{2}\text{ proving }(a_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (a_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'})(a_{1})\).
By propositional extensionality, we obtain \((\exists a_{2}\, :\, \iota,\; (\exists x_{2}\text{ proving }(a_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (a_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'}) \land a_{2} = a_{1}) = (\exists x_{2}\text{ proving }(a_{1} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (a_{1}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'})\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \((\exists x_{2}\, :\, \iota,\; \exists h\text{ proving }(x_{2} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'} \land x_{2} = a_{1}) = (\exists x_{2}\text{ proving }(a_{1} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (a_{1}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'})\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \((\exists x_{2}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\},\; x_{2} \in s^{'} \land x_{2} = a_{1}) = (\exists x_{2}\text{ proving }(a_{1} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (a_{1}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'})\).
Chaining the equalities, we obtain \((a_{1} \in (z \mapsto z)[s^{'}]) = (\exists x_{2}\text{ proving }(a_{1} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (a_{1}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'})\).
Applying the same function to equal arguments, we obtain \((a_{1} \in (z \mapsto z)[s^{'}] \land x_{1} \in t(a_{1})) = ((\exists x_{2}\text{ proving }(a_{1} \in \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}),\; (a_{1}\, :\, \{x^{'}\in \iota \mid x^{'} \ne x\}) \in s^{'}) \land x_{1} \in t(a_{1}))\).

Rewriting with \paperlink{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6269556e696f6e}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:46696e7365742e6d656d5f6269556e696f6e}}, \paperlink{premise:536574436f652e657869737473}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:536574436f652e657869737473}}, \paperlink{premise:66756e657874}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:66756e657874}}, \paperlink{premise:6966665f73656c66}{Prerequisite~P\ref{premise:6966665f73656c66}} and the two identities established above gives \(x_{1} \in \bigcup_{x_{2}\in s^{'}} t(x_{2}) \iff x_{1} \in \bigcup_{i\in (z \mapsto z)[s^{'}]} t(i)\).
\end{proofstep}
The substitution uses the following argument.
\begin{proofstep}
Assume \(\bigcup_{i\in \left(z \mapsto z\right)\left[s^{'}\right]} t(i) = \bigcup_{x_{1}\in s^{'}} t\left(x_{1}\right)\).

Assume \(\bigcup_{x_{1}\in s^{'}} t\left(x_{1}\right) = \bigcup_{x_{1}\in s^{'}} t\left(x_{1}\right)\).

By reflexivity, we obtain \(\left|\bigcup_{x_{1}\in s^{'}} t\left(x_{1}\right)\right|\) heterogeneously equal to itself.

Use the hypothesis \(\bigcup_{i\in \left(z \mapsto z\right)\left[s^{'}\right]} t(i) = \bigcup_{x_{1}\in s^{'}} t\left(x_{1}\right)\).
